\Gamma^{**}(t,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{4})
.
And, instead of the vertices being labeled
\mathcal{E}^{*}_{\text{BF}}(\theta)
, they will be labeled
\mathcal{E}^{*}_{\text{VW}}(\theta)
.

Since the
\mathcal{E}^{*}_{\text{VW}}(\theta)
’s and
\Gamma^{**}(t,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{4})
’s are
\xi
-invariant and therefore, have the same value for all
\xi
, we have BPS worldvolumes of the kind similar to
Fig. 3
.
This time, however, instead of the faces being labeled
\Sigma^{**,**}_{\pm}(t,\tau,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{2})
and
\Gamma^{**}(\tau,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{2})
, they will be labeled
\Sigma^{**,**}_{\pm}(\tau,t,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{4})
and
\Gamma^{**}(t,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{4})
.
And, instead of the edges being labeled
\mathcal{E}^{*}_{\text{BF}}(\theta)
, they will be labeled
\mathcal{E}^{*}_{\text{VW}}(\theta)
.

The 3d Model on
\mathbb{R}^{3}
and an Open Membrane Theory in
\mathfrak{A}_{4}

Thus, like in

§

3.3
, one can understand the 3d gauged sigma model with target space
\mathfrak{A}_{4}
to define an open membrane theory in
\mathfrak{A}_{4}
, whose
effective
worldvolume and boundaries are similar to
Fig. 3
, where
\xi
is the temporal direction, and
\tau
and
t
are the spatial directions.

5.4
Soliton String Theory, the 7d-Spin
(7)
Partition Function, and an FS type
A_{\infty}
-category of
\mathfrak{A}_{4}^{\theta}
-solitons

The 2d Model as a 2d Gauged LG Model

Notice that we can also express (
5.19
) as

\displaystyle\frac{DC^{\mathring{m}}}{D\xi}-k\frac{DC^{\mathring{m}}}{D\tau}-k\left(\frac{D(\tilde{A}_{t})^{\mathring{m}}}{D\xi}-k\frac{D(\tilde{A}_{\tau})^{\mathring{m}}}{D\tau}\right)-jF_{\xi\tau}

\displaystyle=kQ^{\mathring{m}}+kq^{\mathring{m}}\,,

(5.21)

\displaystyle\frac{D\mathcal{A}^{\mathring{m}}}{D\xi}-k_{\theta}\frac{D\mathcal{A}^{\mathring{m}}}{D\tau}

\displaystyle=-R^{\mathring{m}}-r^{\mathring{m}}\,.

In turn, this means that we can express the action of the 2d gauged sigma model with target space
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{t},\mathfrak{A}_{4})
in (
5.7
) as

\displaystyle S_{\text{2d-LG},\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{t},\mathfrak{A}_{4})}

(5.22)

\displaystyle=\int d\tau d\xi\bigg(\bigg|\left(D_{\xi}-kD_{\tau}\right)\left(C^{\mathring{m}}-k(\tilde{A}_{t})^{\mathring{m}}\right)-jF_{\xi\tau}-kQ^{\mathring{m}}-kq^{\mathring{m}}\bigg|^{2}

\displaystyle+\left|(D_{\xi}-k_{\theta}D_{\tau})\mathcal{A}^{\mathring{m}}+R^{\mathring{m}}+r^{\mathring{m}}\right|^{2}+\dots\bigg)

\displaystyle=\int d\tau d\xi\Bigg(\Bigg|\left(D_{\xi}-kD_{\tau}\right)\left(C^{\mathring{m}}-k(\tilde{A}_{t})^{\mathring{m}}\right)-jF_{\xi\tau}+g^{\mathring{m}\bar{\mathring{n}}}_{\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{t},\mathfrak{A}_{4})}\left(\frac{j\zeta}{2}\frac{\partial W_{4}}{\partial C^{\mathring{n}}}\right)^{*}-kg^{\mathring{m}\bar{\mathring{n}}}_{\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{t},\mathfrak{A}_{4})}\left(\frac{j\zeta}{2}\frac{\partial W_{4}}{\partial(\tilde{A}_{t})^{\mathring{n}}}\right)^{*}\Bigg|^{2}

\displaystyle+\left|(D_{\xi}-k_{\theta}D_{\tau})\mathcal{A}^{\mathring{m}}+g^{\mathring{m}\bar{\mathring{n}}}_{\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{t},\mathfrak{A}_{4})}\left(\frac{j\zeta}{2}\frac{\partial W_{4}}{\partial\mathcal{A}^{\mathring{n}}}\right)^{*}\right|^{2}+\dots\Bigg)

\displaystyle=\int d\tau d\xi\left(\left|D_{\rho}\mathcal{A}^{\mathring{m}}\right|^{2}+\left|D_{\rho}C^{\mathring{m}}\right|^{2}+\left|D_{\rho}(\tilde{A}_{t})^{\mathring{m}}\right|^{2}+\left|\frac{\partial W_{4}}{\partial\mathcal{A}^{\mathring{m}}}\right|^{2}+\left|\frac{\partial W_{4}}{\partial C^{\mathring{m}}}\right|^{2}+\left|\frac{\partial W_{4}}{\partial(\tilde{A}_{t})^{\mathring{m}}}\right|^{2}+\left|F_{\xi\tau}\right|^{2}+\dots\right)\,,

where
g_{\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{t},\mathfrak{A}_{4})}
is the metric on
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{t},\mathfrak{A}_{4})
.
In other words, the 2d gauged sigma model with target space
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{t},\mathfrak{A}_{4})
can also be interpreted as a 2d gauged LG model with target space
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{t},\mathfrak{A}_{4})
and a holomorphic superpotential
W_{4}(\mathcal{A},C,\tilde{A}_{t})
.

By setting
d_{\xi}C^{\mathring{m}}=0=d_{\xi}\mathcal{A}^{\mathring{m}}
and
A_{\xi},A_{\tau},(\tilde{A}_{t})^{\mathring{m}}\rightarrow 0
in the expression within the squared terms in (
5.22
), we can read off the LG
\mathcal{M}^{\theta}(\mathbb{R}_{t},\mathfrak{A}_{4})
-soliton equations corresponding to
\sigma^{IJ,KL}_{\pm}(\tau,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{4})
(that re-expresses (
5.10
) with
A_{\xi},A_{\tau},(\tilde{A}_{t})^{\mathring{m}}\rightarrow 0
) as

\displaystyle\frac{dC^{\mathring{m}}}{d\tau}

\displaystyle=-kg^{\mathring{m}\bar{\mathring{n}}}_{\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{t},\mathfrak{A}_{4})}\left(\frac{j\zeta}{2}\frac{\partial W_{4}}{\partial C^{\mathring{n}}}\right)^{*}_{\tilde{A}_{t}=0}-g^{\mathring{m}\bar{\mathring{n}}}_{\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{t},\mathfrak{A}_{4})}\left(\frac{j\zeta}{2}\frac{\partial W_{4}}{\partial(\tilde{A}_{t})^{\mathring{n}}}\right)^{*}_{\tilde{A}_{t}=0}\,,

(5.23)

\displaystyle\frac{d\mathcal{A}^{\mathring{m}}}{d\tau}

\displaystyle=-k_{\theta}g^{\mathring{m}\bar{\mathring{n}}}_{\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{t},\mathfrak{A}_{4})}\left(\frac{j\zeta}{2}\frac{\partial W_{4}}{\partial\mathcal{A}^{\mathring{n}}}\right)^{*}_{\tilde{A}_{t}=0}\,.

By setting
d_{\tau}C^{\mathring{m}}=0=d_{\tau}\mathcal{A}^{\mathring{m}}
in (
5.23
), we get the LG
\mathcal{M}^{\theta}(\mathbb{R}_{t},\mathfrak{A}_{4})
-soliton endpoint equations corresponding to
\sigma^{IJ,KL}(\pm\infty,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{4})
(that re-expresses (
5.15
)) as

\displaystyle kg^{\mathring{m}\bar{\mathring{n}}}_{\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{t},\mathfrak{A}_{4})}\left(\frac{j\zeta}{2}\frac{\partial W_{4}}{\partial C^{\mathring{n}}}\right)^{*}_{\tilde{A}_{t}=0}+g^{\mathring{m}\bar{\mathring{n}}}_{\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{t},\mathfrak{A}_{4})}\left(\frac{j\zeta}{2}\frac{\partial W_{4}}{\partial(\tilde{A}_{t})^{\mathring{n}}}\right)^{*}_{\tilde{A}_{t}=0}

\displaystyle=0\,,

(5.24)

\displaystyle k_{\theta}g^{\mathring{m}\bar{\mathring{n}}}_{\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{t},\mathfrak{A}_{4})}\left(\frac{j\zeta}{2}\frac{\partial W_{4}}{\partial\mathcal{A}^{\mathring{n}}}\right)^{*}_{\tilde{A}_{t}=0}

\displaystyle=0\,.

Recall from the end of

§

5.2
that we are only considering certain
CY_{2}
such that the
\theta
-deformed VW configurations are isolated and non-degenerate.
Next, recall also that such configurations will correspond to the endpoints of the
\mathfrak{A}_{4}^{\theta}
-solitons; therefore, just like their endpoints, these
\mathfrak{A}_{4}^{\theta}
-solitons would be isolated and non-degenerate.
As these
\mathfrak{A}_{4}^{\theta}
-solitons will correspond, in the 2d gauged sigma model, to the endpoints of the
\mathcal{M}^{\theta}(\mathbb{R}_{t},\mathfrak{A}_{4})
-solitons, i.e.,
\sigma^{IJ,KL}(\pm\infty,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{4})
, this means the latter would also be isolated and non-degenerate.
Thus, from their definition in (
5.24
) which tells us that they are critical points of
W_{4}(\mathcal{A},C,\tilde{A}_{t})
, we conclude that
W_{4}(\mathcal{A},C,\tilde{A}_{t})
can be regarded as a holomorphic Morse function in
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{t},\mathfrak{A}_{4})
.

Just like in

§

3.4
, an LG
\mathcal{M}^{\theta}(\mathbb{R}_{t},\mathfrak{A}_{4})
-soliton defined in (
5.23
) maps to a straight line in the complex
W_{4}
-plane that starts and ends at the critical values
W_{4}^{IJ}(\theta)\equiv W_{4}(\sigma^{IJ}(-\infty,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{4}))
and
W_{4}^{KL}(\theta)\equiv W_{4}(\sigma^{KL}(+\infty,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{4}))
, respectively, where its slope depends on
\theta
(via
\zeta
).
We shall also assume that
\text{Re}(W_{4}^{IJ}(\theta))<\text{Re}(W_{4}^{KL}(\theta))
and that
I\neq J
,
J\neq K
, and
K\neq L
.

The 2d Gauged LG Model as an LG SQM

With suitable rescalings, we can recast (
5.22
) as a 1d LG SQM (that re-expresses (
5.9
)), where its action will be given by
48
48

48

In the following expression, we have integrated out
A_{\xi}
and omitted the fields corresponding to the finite-energy gauge fields
A_{\{t,\tau\}}
(as explained in
footnote 14
).

\displaystyle S_{\text{2d-LG SQM},\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{t},\mathfrak{A}_{4}))}=\int d\xi\Bigg(

\displaystyle\left|\left(\frac{dC^{\mathring{u}}}{d\xi}-k\frac{d\breve{A}^{\mathring{u}}}{d\xi}\right)+g_{\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{t},\mathfrak{A}_{4}))}^{\mathring{u}\mathring{v}}\left(\frac{\partial H_{4}}{\partial C^{\mathring{v}}}-k\frac{\partial H_{4}}{\partial\breve{A}^{\mathring{v}}}\right)\right|^{2}

(5.25)

\displaystyle+\left|\frac{d\mathcal{A}^{\mathring{u}}}{d\xi}+g_{\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{t},\mathfrak{A}_{4}))}^{\mathring{u}\mathring{v}}\frac{\partial H_{4}}{\partial\mathcal{A}^{\mathring{v}}}\right|^{2}+\dots\Bigg)\,,

where
H_{4}(\mathcal{A},C,\breve{A})
is the
real-valued
potential in
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{t},\mathfrak{A}_{4}))
, and the subscript “2d-LG SQM,
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{t},\mathfrak{A}_{4}))
” is to specify that it is a 1d SQM in
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{t},\mathfrak{A}_{4}))
obtained from the equivalent 2d LG model.
We will also refer to this
1d
LG SQM as “2d-LG SQM” in the rest of this subsection.

The 2d-LG SQM will localize onto configurations that
simultaneously
set to zero the LHS and RHS of the expression within the squared terms in (
5.25
).
In other words, it will localize onto
\xi
-invariant critical points of
H_{4}(\mathcal{A},C,\breve{A})
that will correspond, when
A_{\xi},A_{\tau},(\tilde{A}_{t})^{\mathring{m}}\rightarrow 0
, to the LG
\mathcal{M}^{\theta}(\mathbb{R}_{t},\mathfrak{A}_{4})
-solitons defined by (
5.23
).
For our choice of
CY_{2}
, the LG
\mathcal{M}^{\theta}(\mathbb{R}_{t},\mathfrak{A}_{4})
-solitons, just like their endpoints, will be isolated and non-degenerate.
Thus,
H_{4}(\mathcal{A},C,\breve{A})
can be regarded as a real-valued Morse functional in
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{t},\mathfrak{A}_{4}))
.

Morphisms from
\gamma^{IJ}(\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{4})
to
\gamma^{KL}(\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{4})
as Floer Homology Classes of Intersecting Thimbles

Repeating here the analysis in

§

3.4
with (
5.25
) as the action of the 2d-LG SQM, we find that we can interpret the LG
\mathcal{M}^{\theta}(\mathbb{R}_{t},\mathfrak{A}_{4})
-soliton solution
\sigma^{IJ,KL}_{\pm}(\tau,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{4})
as intersections of thimbles.
Specifically, a
\sigma^{IJ,KL}_{\pm}(\tau,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{4})
-soliton pair, whose left and right endpoints correspond to
\gamma^{IJ}(\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{4})
and
\gamma^{KL}(\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{4})
, respectively, can be identified as a pair of intersection points
p^{IJ,KL}_{\text{VW},\pm}(\theta)\in S^{IJ}_{\text{VW}}\bigcap S^{KL}_{\text{VW}}
of a left and right thimble in the fiber space over the line segment
[W^{IJ}_{4}(\theta),W^{KL}_{4}(\theta)]
.

This means that the 2d-LG SQM in
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{t},\mathfrak{A}_{4}))
with action (
5.25
), will physically realize a Floer homology that we shall name an
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{t},\mathfrak{A}_{4})
-LG Floer homology.
The chains of the
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{t},\mathfrak{A}_{4})
-LG Floer complex will be generated by LG
\mathcal{M}^{\theta}(\mathbb{R}_{t},\mathfrak{A}_{4})
-solitons which we can identify with
p^{**,**}_{\text{VW},\pm}(\theta)
, and the
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{t},\mathfrak{A}_{4})
-LG Floer differential will be realized by the flow lines governed by the gradient flow equations satisfied by the
\xi
-varying configurations which set the expression within the squared terms in (
5.25
) to zero.
The partition function of the 2d-LG SQM in
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{t},\mathfrak{A}_{4}))
will then be given by
49
49

49

The ‘
\theta
’ label is omitted in the LHS of the following expression (as explained in
footnote 20
).

\mathcal{Z}_{\text{2d-LG SQM},\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{t},\mathfrak{A}_{4}))}(G)=\sum^{n}_{I\neq J\neq K\neq L=1}\,\sum_{p^{IJ,KL}_{\text{VW},\pm}\in S^{IJ}_{\text{VW}}\cap S^{KL}_{\text{VW}}}\text{HF}^{G}_{d_{v}}\left(p^{IJ,KL}_{\text{VW},\pm}(\theta)\right)\,.

(5.26)

Here, the contribution
\text{HF}^{G}_{d_{v}}(p^{IJ,KL}_{\text{VW},\pm}(\theta))
can be identified with a homology class in an
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{t},\mathfrak{A}_{4})
-LG Floer homology generated by intersection points of thimbles.
These intersection points represent LG
\mathcal{M}^{\theta}(\mathbb{R}_{t},\mathfrak{A}_{4})
-solitons defined by (
5.23
), whose endpoints correspond to
\theta
-deformed, non-constant paths in
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{t},\mathfrak{A}_{4})
defined by (
5.24
).
The degree of each chain in the complex is
d_{v}
, and is counted by the number of outgoing flow lines from the fixed critical points of
H_{4}(\mathcal{A},C,\breve{A})
in
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{t},\mathfrak{A}_{4}))
which can also be identified as
p^{IJ,KL}_{\text{VW},\pm}(\theta)
.

Therefore,
\mathcal{Z}_{\text{2d-LG SQM},\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{t},\mathfrak{A}_{4}))}(G)
in (
5.26
) is a sum of LG
\mathcal{M}^{\theta}(\mathbb{R}_{t},\mathfrak{A}_{4})
-solitons defined by (
5.23
) with endpoints (
5.24
), or equivalently,
\sigma^{IJ,KL}_{\pm}(\tau,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{4})
-solitons defined by (
5.10
) (with
A_{\xi},A_{\tau},(\tilde{A}_{t})^{\mathring{m}}\rightarrow 0
) with endpoints (
5.15
), whose start and end correspond to the non-constant paths
\gamma^{IJ}(\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{4})
and
\gamma^{KL}(\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{4})
, respectively.
In other words, we can write

\text{CF}_{\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{t},\mathfrak{A}_{4})}\left(\gamma^{IJ}(\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{4}),\gamma^{KL}(\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{4})\right)_{\pm}=\text{HF}^{G}_{d_{v}}\left(p^{IJ,KL}_{\text{VW},\pm}(\theta)\right)

(5.27)

where
\text{CF}_{\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{t},\mathfrak{A}_{4})}(\gamma^{IJ}(\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{4}),\gamma^{KL}(\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{4}))_{\pm}
is a vector representing a
\sigma^{IJ,KL}_{\pm}(\tau,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{4})
-soliton, such that
\text{Re}(W^{IJ}_{4}(\theta))<\text{Re}(W^{KL}_{4}(\theta))
.
This will lead us to the following one-to-one identification
50
50

50

The ‘
\theta
’ label is omitted in the following expression (as explained in
footnote 21
).

\boxed{\text{Hom}\left(\gamma^{IJ}(\mathfrak{A}_{4}),\gamma^{KL}(\mathfrak{A}_{4})\right)_{\pm}\Longleftrightarrow\text{HF}^{G}_{d_{v}}\left(p^{IJ,KL}_{\text{VW},\pm}\right)}

(5.28)

where the RHS is proportional to the identity class when
I=K
and
J=L
, and zero when
I\leftrightarrow K
and
J\leftrightarrow L
(since the
\sigma^{IJ,KL}_{\pm}(\tau,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{4})
-soliton only moves in one direction from
\gamma^{IJ}(\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{4})
to
\gamma^{KL}(\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{4})
).

Soliton String Theory from the 2d LG Model

Just like in

§

3.4
, the 2d gauged LG model in
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{t},\mathfrak{A}_{4})
with action (
5.22
) can be interpreted as a soliton string theory in
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{t},\mathfrak{A}_{4})
.
The dynamics of this soliton string theory in
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{t},\mathfrak{A}_{4})
will be governed by the BPS worldsheet equations of (
5.21
), where
(\mathcal{A}^{\mathring{m}},C^{\mathring{m}},(\tilde{A}_{t})^{\mathring{m}})
are scalars on the worldsheet corresponding to the holomorphic coordinates of
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{t},\mathfrak{A}_{4})
.
At an arbitrary instant in time whence
d_{\xi}\mathcal{A}^{\mathring{m}}=d_{\xi}C^{\mathring{m}}=0=d_{\xi}(\tilde{A}_{t})^{\mathring{m}}=d_{\xi}A_{\tau}
in (
5.21
), the dynamics of
(\mathcal{A}^{\mathring{m}},C^{\mathring{m}},(\tilde{A}_{\tau})^{\mathring{m}})
and the 2d gauge fields
(A_{\tau},A_{\xi})
along
\tau
will be governed by the soliton equations

\displaystyle\frac{d(\tilde{A}_{t})^{\mathring{m}}}{d\tau}+k\frac{dC^{\mathring{m}}}{d\tau}-j\frac{dA_{\xi}}{d\tau}=

\displaystyle[A_{\xi}-kA_{\tau},C^{\mathring{m}}-k(\tilde{A}_{t})^{\mathring{m}}]+j[A_{\tau},A_{\xi}]

(5.29)

\displaystyle+g^{\mathring{m}\bar{\mathring{n}}}_{\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{t},\mathfrak{A}_{4})}\left(\frac{j\zeta}{2}\frac{\partial W_{4}}{\partial C^{\mathring{n}}}\right)^{*}-kg^{\mathring{m}\bar{\mathring{n}}}_{\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{t},\mathfrak{A}_{4})}\left(\frac{j\zeta}{2}\frac{\partial W_{4}}{\partial(\tilde{A}_{t})^{\mathring{n}}}\right)^{*}\,,

\displaystyle k_{\theta}\frac{d\mathcal{A}^{\mathring{m}}}{d\tau}=

\displaystyle[A_{\xi}-k_{\theta}A_{\tau},\mathcal{A}^{\mathring{m}}]+g^{\mathring{m}\bar{\mathring{n}}}_{\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{t},\mathfrak{A}_{4})}\left(\frac{j\zeta}{2}\frac{\partial W_{4}}{\partial\mathcal{A}^{\mathring{n}}}\right)^{*}\,.

The Normalized 7d-Spin
(7)
Partition Function, LG
\mathcal{M}^{\theta}(\mathbb{R}_{t},\mathfrak{A}_{4})
-soliton String Scattering, and Maps of an
A_{\infty}
-structure

Since our 7d-Spin
(7)
theory, which was derived from Spin
(7)
theory, is also semi-classical, its normalized 7d partition function will also be a sum over tree-level scattering amplitudes of the LG
\mathcal{M}^{\theta}(\mathbb{R}_{t},\mathfrak{A}_{4})
-soliton strings defined by (
5.23
).
The BPS worldsheet underlying such a tree-level scattering is similar to
Fig. 4
, where instead of the endpoints of each string being labeled
\gamma^{**}(\mathfrak{A}_{2})
, they will now be labeled
\gamma^{**}(\mathfrak{A}_{4})
.

In other words, we can, like in (
3.49
), express the normalized 7d-Spin
(7)
partition function as

\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}_{\text{7d-Spin}(7),CY_{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}(G)=\sum_{\mathfrak{N}_{n}}\mu^{\mathfrak{N}_{n}}_{\mathfrak{A}_{4}}\,,\qquad\mathfrak{N}_{n}=1,2,\dots,\left\lfloor\frac{n-2}{2}\right\rfloor

(5.30)

where each

\boxed{\mu^{\mathfrak{N}_{n}}_{\mathfrak{A}_{4}}:\bigotimes_{i=1}^{\mathfrak{N}_{n}}\text{Hom}\left(\gamma^{I_{2i-1}I_{2i}}(\mathfrak{A}_{4}),\gamma^{I_{2(i+1)-1}I_{2(i+1)}}(\mathfrak{A}_{4})\right)_{-}\longrightarrow\text{Hom}\left(\gamma^{I_{1}I_{2}}(\mathfrak{A}_{4}),\gamma^{I_{2\mathfrak{N}_{n}+1}I_{2\mathfrak{N}_{n}+2}}(\mathfrak{A}_{4})\right)_{+}}

(5.31)

is a scattering amplitude of
\mathfrak{N}_{n}
incoming LG
\mathcal{M}^{\theta}(\mathbb{R}_{t},\mathfrak{A}_{4})
-soliton strings
\text{Hom}(\gamma^{I_{1}I_{2}}(\mathfrak{A}_{4}),\gamma^{I_{3}I_{4}}(\mathfrak{A}_{4}))_{-}
,
\dots
,
\text{Hom}(\gamma^{I_{2\mathfrak{N}_{n}-1}I_{2\mathfrak{N}_{n}}}(\mathfrak{A}_{4}),\gamma^{I_{2\mathfrak{N}_{n}+1}I_{2\mathfrak{N}_{n}+2}}(\mathfrak{A}_{4}))_{-}
, and a single outgoing LG
\mathcal{M}^{\theta}(\mathbb{R}_{t},\mathfrak{A}_{4})
-soliton string
\text{Hom}(\gamma^{I_{1}I_{2}}(\mathfrak{A}_{4}),\gamma^{I_{2\mathfrak{N}_{n}+1}I_{2\mathfrak{N}_{n}+2}}(\mathfrak{A}_{4}))_{+}
, with left and right boundaries as labeled, whose underlying worldsheet can be regarded as a disc with
\mathfrak{N}_{n}+1
vertex operators at the boundary.
In short,
\mu^{\mathfrak{N}_{n}}_{\mathfrak{A}_{4}}
counts pseudoholomorphic discs with
\mathfrak{N}_{n}+1
punctures at the boundary that are mapped to
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{t},\mathfrak{A}_{4})
according to the BPS worldsheet equations (
5.21
).

Just as in

§

3.4
, the collection of
\mu^{\mathfrak{N}_{n}}_{\mathfrak{A}_{4}}
maps in (
5.31
) can be regarded as composition maps defining an
A_{\infty}
-structure.

An FS type
A_{\infty}
-category of
\mathfrak{A}_{4}^{\theta}
-solitons

Altogether, this means that the normalized partition function of 7d-Spin
(7)
theory on
CY_{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
as expressed in (
5.30
), manifests a
novel
FS type
A_{\infty}
-category defined by the
\mu^{\mathfrak{N}_{n}}_{\mathfrak{A}_{4}}
maps (
5.31
) and the one-to-one identification (
5.28
), where the
\mathfrak{N}_{n}+1
number of objects
\big\{\gamma^{I_{1}I_{2}}(\mathfrak{A}_{4}),\gamma^{I_{3}I_{4}}(\mathfrak{A}_{4}),\dots,

\gamma^{I_{2\mathfrak{N}_{n}+1}I_{2\mathfrak{N}_{n}+2}}(\mathfrak{A}_{4})\big\}
correspond to
\mathfrak{A}_{4}^{\theta}
-solitons with endpoints themselves corresponding to (
\theta
-deformed) VW configurations on
CY_{2}
!

5.5
Soliton Membrane Theory, the 7d-Spin
(7)
Partition Function, and a Fueter type
A_{\infty}
-2-category 2-categorifying the HW Floer Homology of
CY_{2}

Note that we can also express (
5.20
) (corresponding to (
5.19
) in the 2d gauged sigma model) as

\displaystyle i\frac{DC^{\mathring{a}}}{D\xi}+j\frac{DC^{\mathring{a}}}{D\tau}+k\frac{DC^{\mathring{a}}}{Dt}+iF_{t\tau}+jF_{\xi t}+kF_{\tau\xi}

\displaystyle=-jq^{\mathring{a}}\,,

(5.32)

\displaystyle ie^{-i\theta}\frac{D\mathcal{A}^{\mathring{a}}}{D\xi}+j\frac{D\mathcal{A}^{\mathring{a}}}{D\tau}+k\frac{D\mathcal{A}^{\mathring{a}}}{Dt}

\displaystyle=-ie^{-i\theta}r^{\mathring{a}}\,,

which are non-constant,
\theta
-deformed, gauged Fueter equations for the
(\mathcal{A}^{\mathring{a}},C^{\mathring{a}})
fields (that will correspond to (
5.21
) in the 2d gauged LG model).
In turn, this means that we can express the action of the 3d gauged sigma model with target space
\mathfrak{A}_{4}
in (
5.5
) as

\displaystyle S_{\text{3d-LG},\mathfrak{A}_{4}}=\frac{1}{e^{2}}\int dtd\tau d\xi\,\bigg(

\displaystyle\left|iD_{\xi}C^{\mathring{a}}+jD_{\tau}C^{\mathring{a}}+kD_{t}C^{\mathring{a}}+iF_{t\tau}+jF_{\xi t}+kF_{\tau\xi}+jq^{\mathring{a}}\right|^{2}

(5.33)

\displaystyle+\left|ie^{-i\theta}D_{\xi}\mathcal{A}^{\mathring{a}}+jD_{\tau}\mathcal{A}^{\mathring{a}}+kD_{t}\mathcal{A}^{\mathring{a}}+ie^{-i\theta}r^{\mathring{a}}\right|^{2}+\dots\bigg)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{e^{2}}\int dtd\tau d\xi\,\bigg(

\displaystyle\left|iD_{\xi}C^{\mathring{a}}+jD_{\tau}C^{\mathring{a}}+kD_{t}C^{\mathring{a}}+iF_{t\tau}+jF_{\xi t}+kF_{\tau\xi}-g_{\mathfrak{A}_{4}}^{\mathring{a}\bar{\mathring{b}}}\left(\frac{j\zeta}{2}\frac{\partial\mathcal{W}_{4}}{\partial C^{\mathring{b}}}\right)^{*}\right|^{2}

\displaystyle+\left|ie^{-i\theta}D_{\xi}\mathcal{A}^{\mathring{a}}+jD_{\tau}\mathcal{A}^{\mathring{a}}+kD_{t}\mathcal{A}^{\mathring{a}}-g_{\mathfrak{A}_{4}}^{\mathring{a}\bar{\mathring{b}}}\left(\frac{i\zeta}{2}\frac{\partial\mathcal{W}_{4}}{\partial\mathcal{A}^{\mathring{b}}}\right)^{*}\right|^{2}+\dots\bigg)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{e^{2}}\int dtd\tau d\xi\,\bigg(

\displaystyle\left|D_{\varrho}\mathcal{A}^{\mathring{a}}\right|^{2}+\left|D_{\varrho}C^{\mathring{a}}\right|^{2}+\left|\frac{\partial\mathcal{W}_{4}}{\partial\mathcal{A}^{\mathring{a}}}\right|^{2}+\left|\frac{\partial\mathcal{W}_{4}}{\partial C^{\mathring{a}}}\right|^{2}+\left|F_{t\tau}\right|^{2}+\left|F_{\xi t}\right|^{2}+\left|F_{\tau\xi}\right|^{2}+\dots\bigg)\,,

where
g_{\mathfrak{A}_{4}}
is the metric on
\mathfrak{A}_{4}
.
In other words, our 3d gauged sigma model can also be interpreted as a 3d gauged LG model in
\mathfrak{A}_{4}
with holomorphic superpotential
\mathcal{W}_{4}(\mathcal{A},C)
.

By setting
d_{\xi}C^{\mathring{a}}=0=d_{\xi}\mathcal{A}^{\mathring{a}}
and
A_{\xi},A_{\tau},A_{t}\rightarrow 0
in the expression within the squared terms in (
5.33
), we can read off the LG
\mathfrak{A}_{4}^{\theta}
-sheet equations corresponding to
\Sigma^{IJ,KL}_{\pm}(\tau,t,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{4})
(that re-expresses (
5.11
) with
A_{\xi},A_{\tau},A_{t}\rightarrow 0
) as

\displaystyle j\frac{dC^{\mathring{a}}}{d\tau}+k\frac{dC^{\mathring{a}}}{dt}

\displaystyle=g_{\mathfrak{A}_{4}}^{\mathring{a}\bar{\mathring{b}}}\left(\frac{j\zeta}{2}\frac{\partial\mathcal{W}_{4}}{\partial C^{\mathring{a}}}\right)^{*}\,,

(5.34)

\displaystyle j\frac{d\mathcal{A}^{\mathring{a}}}{d\tau}+k\frac{d\mathcal{A}^{\mathring{a}}}{dt}

\displaystyle=g_{\mathfrak{A}_{4}}^{\mathring{a}\bar{\mathring{b}}}\left(\frac{i\zeta}{2}\frac{\partial\mathcal{W}_{4}}{\partial\mathcal{A}^{\mathring{a}}}\right)^{*}\,.

By setting
d_{\tau}C^{\mathring{a}}=0=d_{\tau}\mathcal{A}^{\mathring{a}}
in (
5.34
), we can read off the LG
\mathfrak{A}_{4}^{\theta}
-soliton equations corresponding to
\Gamma^{IJ}(t,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{4})
and
\Gamma^{KL}(t,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{4})
, or equivalently, the LG
\mathfrak{A}_{4}^{\theta}
-sheet edge equations corresponding to
\Sigma^{IJ,KL}(\pm\infty,t,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{4})
, (that re-expresses (
5.16
)) as

\displaystyle k\frac{dC^{\mathring{a}}}{dt}

\displaystyle=g_{\mathfrak{A}_{4}}^{\mathring{a}\bar{\mathring{b}}}\left(\frac{j\zeta}{2}\frac{\partial\mathcal{W}_{4}}{\partial C^{\mathring{a}}}\right)^{*}\,,

(5.35)

\displaystyle k\frac{d\mathcal{A}^{\mathring{a}}}{dt}

\displaystyle=g_{\mathfrak{A}_{4}}^{\mathring{a}\bar{\mathring{b}}}\left(\frac{i\zeta}{2}\frac{\partial\mathcal{W}_{4}}{\partial\mathcal{A}^{\mathring{a}}}\right)^{*}\,.

By setting
d_{t}C^{\mathring{a}}=0=d_{t}\mathcal{A}^{\mathring{a}}
in (
5.35
), we can read off the LG
\mathfrak{A}_{4}^{\theta}
-soliton endpoint equations corresponding to
\Gamma^{**}(\pm\infty,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{4})
, or equivalently, the LG
\mathfrak{A}_{4}^{\theta}
-sheet vertex equations corresponding to
\Sigma^{IJ,KL}(\pm\infty,\pm\infty,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{4})
and
\Sigma^{IJ,KL}(\pm\infty,\mp\infty,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{4})
, (that re-expresses (
5.16
) with
d_{t}C^{\mathring{a}}=0=d_{t}\mathcal{A}^{\mathring{a}}
) as

g_{\mathfrak{A}_{4}}^{\mathring{a}\bar{\mathring{b}}}\left(\frac{j\zeta}{2}\frac{\partial\mathcal{W}_{4}}{\partial C^{\mathring{a}}}\right)^{*}=0\,,\qquad g_{\mathfrak{A}_{4}}^{\mathring{a}\bar{\mathring{b}}}\left(\frac{i\zeta}{2}\frac{\partial\mathcal{W}_{4}}{\partial\mathcal{A}^{\mathring{a}}}\right)^{*}=0\,.

(5.36)

Recall from the end of

§

5.2
that we are only considering certain
CY_{2}
such that (the endpoints
\Gamma^{**}(\pm\infty,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{4})
and thus) the LG
\mathfrak{A}_{4}^{\theta}
-solitons, and effectively, (the vertices
\Sigma^{IJ,KL}(\pm\infty,\pm\infty,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{4})
,
\Sigma^{IJ,KL}(\pm\infty,\mp\infty,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{4})
and thus) the LG
\mathfrak{A}_{4}^{\theta}
-sheets, are isolated and non-degenerate.
Therefore, from their definition in (
5.36
) which tells us that they correspond to critical points of
\mathcal{W}_{4}(\mathcal{A},C)
, we conclude that
\mathcal{W}_{4}(\mathcal{A},C)
can be regarded as a holomorphic Morse function in
\mathfrak{A}_{4}
.

Just like in

§

3.5
, this means that an LG
\mathfrak{A}_{4}^{\theta}
-soliton
\Gamma^{IJ}(t,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{4})
defined in (
5.35
) maps to a straight line segment
[\mathcal{W}_{4}^{I}(\theta),\mathcal{W}_{4}^{J}(\theta)]
in the complex
\mathcal{W}_{4}
-plane that starts and ends at critical values
\mathcal{W}^{I}_{4}(\theta)\equiv\mathcal{W}_{4}(\Gamma^{I}(-\infty,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{4}))
and
\mathcal{W}^{J}_{4}(\theta)\equiv\mathcal{W}_{4}(\Gamma^{J}(+\infty,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{4}))
, respectively, where its slope depends on
\theta
(via
\zeta
).
Therefore, an LG
\mathfrak{A}_{4}^{\theta}
-sheet defined in (
5.34
) maps to a quadrilateral in the complex
\mathcal{W}_{4}
-plane, whose edges are the straight line segments that the LG
\mathfrak{A}_{4}^{\theta}
-solitons map to, and whose bottom-left, top-left, bottom-right, and top-right vertices are the critical points
\mathcal{W}^{I}_{4}(\theta)\equiv\mathcal{W}_{4}(\Sigma^{I}(-\infty,-\infty,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{4}))
,
\mathcal{W}^{J}_{4}(\theta)\equiv\mathcal{W}_{4}(\Sigma^{J}(-\infty,+\infty,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{4}))
,
\mathcal{W}^{K}_{4}(\theta)\equiv\mathcal{W}_{4}(\Sigma^{K}(+\infty,-\infty,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{4}))
, and
\mathcal{W}^{L}_{4}(\theta)\equiv

\mathcal{W}_{4}(\Sigma^{L}(+\infty,+\infty,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{4}))
, respectively, where the slope of the straight line segments between each left-right vertex pair depends on
\theta
(via
\zeta
).

We shall also assume that
\text{Re}(\mathcal{W}_{4}^{I}(\theta))<\{\text{Re}(\mathcal{W}_{4}^{J}(\theta)),\text{Re}(\mathcal{W}_{4}^{K}(\theta))\}<\text{Re}(\mathcal{W}_{4}^{L}(\theta))
.

The 3d Gauged LG Model as an LG SQM

Last but not least, after suitable rescalings, we can recast (
5.33
) as a 1d LG SQM (that re-expresses (
5.9
)), where its action will be given by
51
51

51

In the following expression, we have integrated out
A_{\xi}
and omitted the fields corresponding to the finite-energy gauge fields
A_{\{t,\tau\}}
(as explained in
footnote 14
).

\displaystyle S_{\text{3d-LG SQM},\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}^{2},\mathfrak{A}_{4})}=\frac{1}{e^{2}}\int d\xi\Bigg(

\displaystyle\left|i\left(\frac{dC^{\mathring{u}}}{d\xi}-k\frac{d\breve{A}^{\mathring{u}}}{d\xi}\right)+g^{\mathring{u}\mathring{v}}_{\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}^{2},\mathfrak{A}_{4})}\left(\frac{\partial\mathfrak{F}_{4}}{\partial C^{\mathring{v}}}-k\frac{\partial\mathfrak{F}_{4}}{\partial\breve{A}^{\mathring{v}}}\right)\right|^{2}

(5.37)

\displaystyle+\left|ie^{-i\theta}\frac{d\mathcal{A}^{\mathring{u}}}{d\xi}+g^{\mathring{u}\mathring{v}}_{\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}^{2},\mathfrak{A}_{4})}\frac{\partial\mathfrak{F}_{4}}{\partial\mathcal{A}^{\mathring{v}}}\right|^{2}+\dots\Bigg)\,.

Here,
\mathfrak{F}_{4}(\mathcal{A},C,\breve{A})
is the
real-valued
potential in
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}^{2},\mathfrak{A}_{4})
, and the subscript “3d-LG SQM,
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}^{2},\mathfrak{A}_{4})
” is to specify that it is a 1d SQM with target space
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}^{2},\mathfrak{A}_{4})
obtained from the equivalent 3d LG model.
We will also refer to this
1d
LG SQM as “3d-LG SQM” in the rest of this subsection.

The 3d-LG SQM will localize onto configurations that
simultaneously
set to zero the LHS and RHS of the expression within the squared terms in (
5.37
).
In other words, it will localize onto
\xi
-invariant critical points of
\mathfrak{F}_{4}(\mathcal{A},C,\breve{A})
that will correspond, when
A_{\xi},A_{\tau},A_{t}\rightarrow 0
, to the LG
\mathfrak{A}_{4}^{\theta}
-sheets defined by (
5.34
).
For our choice of
CY_{2}
, the LG
\mathfrak{A}_{4}^{\theta}
-sheets, just like their vertices, will be isolated and non-degenerate.
Thus,
\mathfrak{F}_{4}(\mathcal{A},C,\breve{A})
can be regarded as a
real-valued
Morse functional in
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}^{2},\mathfrak{A}_{4})
.

Morphisms between
\mathfrak{A}_{4}^{\theta}
-solitons as Intersection Floer Homology Classes

Repeating here the analysis in

§

3.5
with (
5.37
) as the action of the 3d-LG SQM, we find that we can interpret the LG
\mathfrak{A}_{4}^{\theta}
-soliton solution
\Gamma^{IJ}(t,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{4})
as a thimble-intersection, and the LG
\mathfrak{A}_{4}^{\theta}
-sheet solution
\Sigma^{IJ,KL}_{\pm}(\tau,t,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{4})
as an intersection of thimble-intersections.

Specifically, a
\Gamma^{IJ}(t,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{4})
-soliton, whose bottom and top endpoints correspond to
\mathcal{E}^{I}_{\text{VW}}(\theta)
and
\mathcal{E}^{J}_{\text{VW}}(\theta)
, respectively, can be identified as an intersection point
q^{IJ}_{\text{VW},\pm}(\theta)\in S^{IJ}_{\text{VW}}(\theta)
of a bottom and top thimble in the fiber space over the line segment
[\mathcal{W}_{4}^{I}(\theta),\mathcal{W}_{4}^{J}(\theta)]
.
As a result, a
\Sigma^{IJ,KL}_{\pm}(\tau,t,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{4})
-sheet pair, whose left and right edges correspond to
\Gamma^{IJ}(t,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{4})
and
\Gamma^{KL}(t,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{4})
, respectively, can be identified as a pair of intersection points
\{q^{IJ}_{\text{VW},\pm}(\theta),q^{KL}_{\text{VW},\pm}(\theta)\}\eqqcolon\mathfrak{P}^{IJ,KL}_{\text{VW},\pm}(\theta)\in S^{IJ}_{\text{VW}}(\theta)\bigcap S^{KL}_{\text{VW}}(\theta)
of a left and right thimble-intersection in the fiber space over the quadrilateral with vertices
(\mathcal{W}_{4}^{I}(\theta),\mathcal{W}_{4}^{J}(\theta),\mathcal{W}_{4}^{K}(\theta),\mathcal{W}_{4}^{L}(\theta))
.

At any rate, the 3d-LG SQM in
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}^{2},\mathfrak{A}_{4})
with action (
5.37
) will physically realize a Floer homology that we shall name an
\mathfrak{A}_{4}
-3d-LG Floer homology.
The chains of the
\mathfrak{A}_{4}
-3d-LG Floer complex are generated by LG
\mathfrak{A}_{4}^{\theta}
-sheets which we can thus identify with
\mathfrak{P}^{**,**}_{\text{VW},\pm}(\theta)
, and the
\mathfrak{A}_{4}
-3d-LG Floer differential will be realized by the flow lines governed by the gradient flow equations satisfied by
\xi
-varying configurations which set the expression within the squared terms of (
5.37
) to zero.
The partition function of the 3d-LG SQM in
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}^{2},\mathfrak{A}_{4})
will be given by
52
52

52

The ‘
\theta
’ label is omitted in the LHS of the following expression (as explained in
footnote 20
).

\mathcal{Z}_{\text{3d-LG SQM},\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}^{2},\mathfrak{A}_{4})}(G)=\sum_{I\neq J\neq K\neq L=1}^{n}\,\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathfrak{P}^{IJ,KL}_{\text{VW},\pm}\\
\in S^{IJ}_{\text{VW}}\cap S^{KL}_{\text{VW}}\end{subarray}}\text{HF}^{G}_{d_{v}}\left(\mathfrak{P}^{IJ,KL}_{\text{VW},\pm}(\theta)\right)\,,

(5.38)

where the contribution
\text{HF}^{G}_{d_{v}}(\mathfrak{P}^{IJ,KL}_{\text{VW},\pm}(\theta))
can be identified with a homology class in an
\mathfrak{A}_{4}
-3d-LG Floer homology generated by intersection points of thimble-intersections.
These intersection points represent LG
\mathfrak{A}_{4}^{\theta}
-sheets defined by (
5.34
), whose edges correspond to LG
\mathfrak{A}_{4}^{\theta}
-solitons defined by (
5.35
), and whose vertices defined by (
5.36
) will correspond to
\theta
-deformed VW configurations on
CY_{2}
.
The degree of each chain in the complex is
d_{v}
, and is counted by the number of outgoing flow lines from the fixed critical points of
\mathfrak{F}_{4}(\mathcal{A},C,\breve{A})
in
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}^{2},\mathfrak{A}_{4})
which can also be identified as
\mathfrak{P}^{IJ,KL}_{\text{VW},\pm}(\theta)
.

Therefore,
\mathcal{Z}_{\text{3d-LG SQM},\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}^{2},\mathfrak{A}_{4})}(G)
in (
5.38
) is a sum of LG
\mathfrak{A}_{4}^{\theta}
-sheets defined by (i) (
5.34
) with (ii) edges (

) and (iii) vertices (

), or equivalently,
\Sigma_{\pm}^{IJ,KL}(\tau,t,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{4})
-sheets defined by (i) (

) and (
5.12
) (with
A_{\xi},A_{\tau},A_{t}\rightarrow 0
) with (ii) edges (
5.16
) and (
5.12
), and (iii) vertices (
5.16
) and (
5.12
) (with
d_{t}C^{\mathring{a}}=0=d_{t}\mathcal{A}^{\mathring{a}}
), respectively.
In other words, we can write

\text{CF}_{\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}^{2},\mathfrak{A}_{4})}\left(\Gamma^{IJ}(t,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{4}),\Gamma^{KL}(t,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{4})\right)_{\pm}=\text{HF}^{G}_{d_{v}}\left(\mathfrak{P}^{IJ,KL}_{\text{VW},\pm}(\theta)\right)\,,

(5.39)

where
\text{CF}_{\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}^{2},\mathfrak{A}_{4})}(\Gamma^{IJ}(t,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{4}),\Gamma^{KL}(t,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{4}))_{\pm}
is a vector representing a
\Sigma^{IJ,KL}_{\pm}(\tau,t,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{4})
-sheet, whose left and right edges correspond to
\Gamma^{IJ}(t,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{4})
and
\Gamma^{KL}(t,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{4})
, respectively, and whose bottom-left, top-left, bottom-right, and top-right vertices correspond to
\mathcal{E}^{I}_{\text{VW}}(\theta)
,
\mathcal{E}^{J}_{\text{VW}}(\theta)
,
\mathcal{E}^{K}_{\text{VW}}(\theta)
, and
\mathcal{E}^{L}_{\text{VW}}(\theta)
, respectively, such that
\text{Re}(\mathcal{W}_{4}^{I}(\theta))<\{\text{Re}(\mathcal{W}_{4}^{J}(\theta)),\text{Re}(\mathcal{W}_{4}^{K}(\theta))\}<\text{Re}(\mathcal{W}_{4}^{L}(\theta))
.
This will lead us to the following one-to-one identifications
53
53

53

The ‘
\theta
’ label is omitted in the following expression (as explained in
footnote 21
).

\boxed{\text{Hom}\left(\Gamma^{IJ}(t,\mathfrak{A}_{4}),\Gamma^{KL}(t,\mathfrak{A}_{4})\right)_{\pm}\Longleftrightarrow\text{Hom}\left(\text{Hom}(\mathcal{E}^{I}_{\text{VW}},\mathcal{E}^{J}_{\text{VW}}),\text{Hom}(\mathcal{E}^{K}_{\text{VW}},\mathcal{E}^{L}_{\text{VW}})\right)_{\pm}\Longleftrightarrow\text{HF}^{G}_{d_{v}}\left(\mathfrak{P}^{IJ,KL}_{\text{VW},\pm}\right)}

(5.40)

where the RHS is proportional to the identity class when

and
J=L
, and zero when (i)
I\leftrightarrow K
and
J\leftrightarrow L
(since the
\Sigma^{IJ,KL}(\tau,t,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{4})
-sheet only moves in one direction from
\Gamma^{IJ}(t,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{4})
to
\Gamma^{KL}(t,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{4})
), and (ii)
I\leftrightarrow J
or
K\leftrightarrow L
(since the
\Gamma^{**}(t,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{4})
-solitons only move in one direction from

to
\mathcal{E}^{J}_{\text{VW}}(\theta)
or
\mathcal{E}^{K}_{\text{VW}}(\theta)
to
\mathcal{E}^{L}_{\text{VW}}(\theta)
).

Soliton Membrane Theory from the 3d LG Model

Just like in

§

3.5
, the 3d gauged LG model in
\mathfrak{A}_{4}
with action (
5.33
) can be interpreted as a soliton membrane theory in
\mathfrak{A}_{4}
.
The dynamics of this soliton membrane theory in
\mathfrak{A}_{4}
will be governed by the BPS worldvolume equations of (
5.32
), where
(\mathcal{A}^{\mathring{a}},C^{\mathring{a}})
are scalars on the worldvolume corresponding to the holomorphic coordinates of
\mathfrak{A}_{4}
.
At an arbitrary instant in time whence
d_{\xi}\mathcal{A}^{\mathring{a}}=d_{\xi}C^{\mathring{a}}=0=d_{\xi}A_{\{t,\tau\}}
in (
5.32
), the dynamics of
(\mathcal{A}^{\mathring{a}},C^{\mathring{a}})
and the 3d gauge fields
(A_{t},A_{\tau},A_{\xi})
along
(\tau,t)
will be governed by the membrane equations

\displaystyle j\frac{dC^{\mathring{a}}}{d\tau}+k\frac{dC^{\mathring{a}}}{dt}+i\frac{dA_{\tau}}{dt}-i\frac{dA_{t}}{d\tau}-j\frac{dA_{\xi}}{dt}+k\frac{dA_{\xi}}{d\tau}

\displaystyle=-[iA_{\xi}+jA_{\tau}+kA_{t},C^{\mathring{a}}]-i[A_{t},A_{\tau}]

(5.41)

\displaystyle+[jA_{t}-kA_{\tau},A_{\xi}]+g^{\mathring{a}\bar{\mathring{b}}}_{\mathfrak{A}_{4}}\left(\frac{j\zeta}{2}\frac{\partial\mathcal{W}_{4}}{\partial C^{\mathring{b}}}\right)^{*}\,,

\displaystyle j\frac{d\mathcal{A}^{\mathring{a}}}{d\tau}+k\frac{d\mathcal{A}^{\mathring{a}}}{dt}

\displaystyle=-[ie^{-i\theta}A_{\xi}+jA_{\tau}+kA_{t},\mathcal{A}^{\mathring{a}}]+g^{\mathring{a}\bar{\mathring{b}}}_{\mathfrak{A}_{4}}\left(\frac{i\zeta}{2}\frac{\partial\mathcal{W}_{4}}{\partial\mathcal{A}^{\mathring{a}}}\right)^{*}\,.

The Normalized 7d-Spin
(7)
Partition Function, Soliton Membrane Scattering, and Maps of an
A_{\infty}
-structure

The normalized 7d-Spin
(7)
partition function can be regarded as a sum over tree-level scattering amplitudes of the LG
\mathfrak{A}_{4}^{\theta}
-sheets defined by (
5.34
).
The BPS worldvolume underlying such a tree-level scattering amplitude is similar to
Fig. 5
, where instead of the vertices of each sheet being labeled
\mathcal{E}^{*}_{\text{BF}}
, they will now be labeled
\mathcal{E}^{*}_{\text{VW}}
.

In other words, we can, like in (
3.67
), express the normalized 7d-Spin
(7)
partition function as

\mathcal{\tilde{Z}}_{\text{7d-Spin}(7),CY_{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}(G)=\sum_{\mathfrak{N}_{n}}\varPi_{\mathfrak{A}_{4}}^{\mathfrak{N}_{n}}\,,\qquad\mathfrak{N}_{n}=1,2,\dots,\left\lfloor\frac{n-2}{2}\right\rfloor

(5.42)

where each

\boxed{\begin{aligned} \varPi^{\mathfrak{N}_{n}}_{\mathfrak{A}_{4}}:\bigotimes_{i=1}^{\mathfrak{N}_{n}}&\text{Hom}\left(\text{Hom}\left(\mathcal{E}^{I_{2i-1}}_{\text{VW}},\mathcal{E}^{I_{2i}}_{\text{VW}}\right),\text{Hom}\left(\mathcal{E}^{I_{2(i+1)-1}}_{\text{VW}},\mathcal{E}^{I_{2(i+1)}}_{\text{VW}}\right)\right)_{-}\\
&\longrightarrow\text{Hom}\left(\text{Hom}\left(\mathcal{E}^{I_{1}}_{\text{VW}},\mathcal{E}^{I_{2}}_{\text{VW}}\right),\text{Hom}\left(\mathcal{E}^{I_{2\mathfrak{N}_{n}+1}}_{\text{VW}},\mathcal{E}^{I_{2\mathfrak{N}_{n}+2}}_{\text{VW}}\right)\right)_{+}\end{aligned}}

(5.43)

is a scattering amplitude of
\mathfrak{N}_{n}
incoming LG
\mathfrak{A}_{4}^{\theta}
-soliton membranes
\text{Hom}\Big(\text{Hom}\big(\mathcal{E}^{I_{1}}_{\text{VW}},\mathcal{E}^{I_{2}}_{\text{VW}}\big),\text{Hom}\big(\mathcal{E}^{I_{3}}_{\text{VW}},\mathcal{E}^{I_{4}}_{\text{VW}}\big)\Big)_{-}
,
\dots
,
\text{Hom}\Big(\text{Hom}\big(\mathcal{E}^{I_{2\mathfrak{N}_{n}-1}}_{\text{VW}},\mathcal{E}^{I_{2\mathfrak{N}_{n}}}_{\text{VW}}\big),\text{Hom}\big(\mathcal{E}^{I_{2\mathfrak{N}_{n}+1}}_{\text{VW}},\mathcal{E}^{I_{2\mathfrak{N}_{n}+2}}_{\text{VW}}\big)\Big)_{-}
, and a single outgoing LG
\mathfrak{A}_{4}^{\theta}
-soliton membrane
\text{Hom}\Big(\text{Hom}(\mathcal{E}^{I_{1}}_{\text{VW}},\mathcal{E}^{I_{2}}_{\text{VW}}),\text{Hom}(\mathcal{E}^{I_{2\mathfrak{N}_{n}+1}}_{\text{VW}},\mathcal{E}^{I_{2\mathfrak{N}_{n}+2}}_{\text{VW}})\Big)_{+}
, with vertices as labeled.

Just as in

§

3.5
, the collection of
\varPi^{\mathfrak{N}_{n}}_{\mathfrak{A}_{4}}
maps in (
5.43
) which involve 2-morphisms, can also be regarded as composition maps defining an
A_{\infty}
-structure of a 2-category whose
n
objects
\{\mathcal{E}^{1}_{\text{VW}},\mathcal{E}^{2}_{\text{VW}},\dots,\mathcal{E}^{n}_{\text{VW}}\}
correspond to (
\theta
-deformed) VW configurations on
CY_{2}
.

A Fueter type
A_{\infty}
-2-category 2-categorifying the HW Floer Homology of
CY_{2}

As VW configurations on
CY_{2}
are known to generate the HW Floer homology of
CY_{2}
which is itself a 0-category, this 2-category is a 2-categorification of the said Floer homology.

Altogether, this means that the normalized partition function of 7d-Spin
(7)
theory on
CY_{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
, as expressed in (
5.42
), manifests a
novel
Fueter type
A_{\infty}
-2-category, defined by the maps (
5.43
) and the identifications (
5.40
), which 2-categorifies the HW Floer homology of
CY_{2}
!

An Equivalence Between a Fueter type
A_{\infty}
-2-category and an FS type
A_{\infty}
-category

Recall from

§

5.4
that the normalized partition function of 7d-Spin
(7)
theory on
CY_{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
also manifests the FS type
A_{\infty}
-category of
\mathfrak{A}_{4}^{\theta}
-solitons.
This means that we have a
novel
equivalence between the Fueter type
A_{\infty}
-2-category 2-categorifying the HW Floer homology of
CY_{2}
and the FS type
A_{\infty}
-category of
\mathfrak{A}_{4}^{\theta}
-solitons!

A Cauchy-Riemann-Fueter type
A_{\infty}
-3-category of Four-Manifolds

In this section, we will study Spin
(7)
theory on
\text{Spin}(7)=M_{4}\times\mathbb{R}^{4}
, where
M_{4}=CY_{2}
.
We will recast it as a 4d gauged LG model on
\mathbb{R}^{4}
, a 2d gauged LG model on
\mathbb{R}^{2}
, or a 1d LG SQM.
Following the approach in

§

4
, we will, via the 8d Spin
(7)
partition function and its equivalent 2d gauged LG model, be able to physically realize a novel FS type
A_{\infty}
-category of sheets whose vertices correspond to VW configurations on
CY_{2}
that generate a HW Floer homology.
Similarly, via the 8d Spin
(7)
partition function and its equivalent 4d gauged LG model, we will be able to also physically realize a novel Cauchy-Riemann-Fueter
A_{\infty}
-3-category that 3-categorifies the HW Floer homology of
CY_{2}
.

6.1
Spin
(7)
Theory on
CY_{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{4}
as a 4d Model on
\mathbb{R}^{4}
, 2d Model on
\mathbb{R}^{2}
, or 1d SQM

Let us now specialize our Spin
(7)
-manifold to one which is actually a product manifold containing an
\mathbb{R}^{4}
submanifold within.
One such possibility is a Spin
(7)
-manifold of the form
\text{Spin}(7)=M_{4}\times\mathbb{R}^{4}
, where
M_{4}
is a closed and compact hyperkähler four-manifold, i.e.,
M_{4}=CY_{2}

[
7
,
5
]
.
We will consider this case, and study Spin
(7)
theory on
\text{Spin}(7)=CY_{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{4}
.

Spin
(7)
Theory on
CY_{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{4}

We will re-use the
(t,\tau,\xi,y)
labels from

§

4
for the
(x^{0},x^{1},x^{2},x^{3})
coordinates of
\mathbb{R}^{4}
.
Exploiting the self-duality of the
F^{+}
’s in the Spin
(7)
instanton equation, we can express the action of Spin
(7)
theory in (
2.7
) on
CY_{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{4}
as

S_{\text{Spin}(7),CY_{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{4}}=\frac{1}{e^{2}}\int_{CY_{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{4}}dtd\tau d\xi dyd^{4}x\,\mathrm{Tr}\left(|F_{y\alpha}^{+}|^{2}+|F_{yt}^{+}|^{2}+|F_{y\tau}^{+}|^{2}+|F_{y\xi}^{+}|^{2}+\dots\right)\,,

(6.1)

where
x^{\alpha}
for
\alpha\in\{4,5,6,7\}
are the coordinates of
CY_{2}
.

The conditions the bosons that minimize the action (
6.1
), i.e., the BPS equations of Spin
(7)
theory on
CY_{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{4}
, are easily identified by setting to zero the expression within the squared terms therein, i.e.,

\displaystyle F_{y\alpha}-IF_{\xi\alpha}-JF_{t\alpha}-KF_{\tau\alpha}

\displaystyle=0\,,

(6.2)

\displaystyle F_{y\xi}+F_{\tau t}+\omega^{\alpha\beta}_{(I)}F_{\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle F_{yt}+F_{\xi\tau}+\omega^{\alpha\beta}_{(J)}F_{\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle F_{y\tau}+F_{t\xi}+\omega^{\alpha\beta}_{(K)}F_{\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle=0\,,

where
(I,J,K)
are the three complex structures of the hyperkähler
CY_{2}
whose actions on the cotangent bases of
CY_{2}
, i.e.,
e_{\alpha}
, are defined as

\displaystyle Ie_{4}

\displaystyle=-e_{5}\,,

\displaystyle\qquad Ie_{6}

\displaystyle=-e_{7}\,,

(6.3)

\displaystyle Je_{4}

\displaystyle=e_{7}\,,

\displaystyle\qquad Je_{5}

\displaystyle=e_{6}\,,

\displaystyle Ke_{4}

\displaystyle=e_{6}\,,

\displaystyle\qquad Ke_{5}

\displaystyle=-e_{7}\,,

and
\omega_{(I/J/K)}
is the Kähler two-form w.r.t.
(I/J/K)
.

Choosing the
I
-complex structure, i.e., using complex coordinates
z^{\kappa}
defined as
z^{1}=x^{5}+ix^{4}
and
z^{2}=x^{7}+ix^{6}
that are holomorphic w.r.t.
I
, we can express (
6.2
) as

\displaystyle(D_{y}-ID_{\xi}-JD_{t}-KD_{\tau})\mathcal{A}^{(I)}_{\kappa}+I(F_{y\xi}+F_{\tau t})+J(F_{yt}+F_{\xi\tau})+K(F_{y\tau}+F_{t\xi})

(6.4)

\displaystyle=\partial^{(I)}_{\kappa}(A_{y}-IA_{\xi}-JA_{t}-KA_{\tau})-2I\omega^{\kappa\bar{\lambda}}_{(I)}\mathcal{F}^{(I)}_{\kappa\bar{\lambda}}+4K\varepsilon^{\kappa\lambda}_{(I)}\mathcal{F}^{(I)}_{\kappa\lambda}\,,

where
\mathcal{A}^{(I)}_{\kappa}\in\Omega^{(1,0)}(CY_{2},\text{ad}(G))\otimes\Omega^{0}(\mathbb{R}^{4},\text{ad}(G))
is a holomorphic gauge connection w.r.t.
I
;

\mathcal{F}^{(I)}_{\kappa\lambda}
are components of the
(2,0)
-form field strength in
\mathcal{A}^{(I)}
;

\mathcal{F}^{(I)}_{\kappa\bar{\lambda}}
are components of the
(1,1)
-form field strength in
\mathcal{A}^{(I)}
and
\bar{\mathcal{A}}^{(I)}
(its complex conjugate that is anti-holomorphic w.r.t
I
);

\partial^{(I)}_{\kappa}
is the holomorphic derivative w.r.t
I
;
and
\varepsilon^{\kappa\lambda}_{(I)}
are components of the holomorphic symplectic form w.r.t.
I
.

Next, note that we are physically free to rotate the
(t,\tau)
and
(\xi,y)
-planes of
\mathbb{R}^{4}
by an angle
\theta
, whence (
6.4
) becomes
54
54

54

If the complex structure chosen was, instead,
J
or
K
, (
6.4
) would still have the same expression (with the “
(I)
” label and the complex structures multiplying
\omega^{\kappa\bar{\lambda}}
and
\varepsilon^{\kappa\lambda}
replaced appropriately (up to a sign)).
Since the form of the expression is the same no matter the choice of the complex structure, we will choose to work in the
I
-complex structure in the rest of this section and omit the “
(I)
” label in the expressions hereafter.

\displaystyle(D_{y}-ID_{\xi}-JD_{t}-KD_{\tau})\mathcal{A}_{\kappa}+I(F_{y\xi}+F_{\tau t})+J(F_{yt}+F_{\xi\tau})+K(F_{y\tau}+F_{t\xi})

(6.5)

\displaystyle=\partial_{\kappa}(A_{y}-IA_{\xi}-JA_{t}-KA_{\tau})-2Ie^{-I\theta}\omega^{\kappa\bar{\lambda}}\mathcal{F}_{\kappa\bar{\lambda}}+4K_{\theta}\varepsilon^{\kappa\lambda}\mathcal{F}_{\kappa\lambda}\,,

where
K_{\theta}\coloneqq e^{I\theta/2}Ke^{-I\theta/2}
.
This allows us to write the action for Spin
(7)
theory on
CY_{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{4}
as

\displaystyle S_{\text{Spin}(7),CY_{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{4}}

(6.6)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{e^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}dtd\tau d\xi dy\int_{CY_{2}}|dz|^{4}\,\mathrm{Tr}\Bigg(\bigg|(D_{y}-ID_{\xi}-JD_{t}-KD_{\tau})\mathcal{A}_{\kappa}+I(F_{y\xi}+F_{\tau t})+J(F_{yt}+F_{\xi\tau})

\displaystyle+K(F_{y\tau}+F_{t\xi})+r_{\kappa}+p+q\bigg|^{2}+\dots\Bigg)\,,

where

r_{\kappa}=-\partial_{\kappa}(A_{y}-IA_{\xi}-JA_{t}-KA_{\tau})\,,\qquad p=2Ie^{-I\theta}\omega^{\kappa\bar{\lambda}}\mathcal{F}_{\kappa\bar{\lambda}}\,,\qquad q=-4K_{\theta}\varepsilon^{\kappa\lambda}\mathcal{F}_{\kappa\lambda}\,.

(6.7)

Spin
(7)
Theory as a 4d Model

After suitable rescalings, we can recast (
6.6
) as a 4d model on
\mathbb{R}^{4}
, where its action now reads
55
55

55

Just like in
footnote 11
, to arrive at the following expression, we have (i) employed Stokes’ theorem and the fact that
CY_{2}
has no boundary to omit terms with
\partial_{\kappa}A_{\{t,\tau,\xi,y\}}
as they will vanish when integrated over
CY_{2}
, and (ii) integrated out the scalar field
\mathfrak{g}_{4}(p)=2Ie^{-I\theta}\omega^{a\bar{b}}\mathcal{F}_{a\bar{b}}
, whose contribution to the action is
|\mathfrak{g}_{4}(p)|^{2}
.

\displaystyle S_{\text{4d},\mathfrak{A}_{4}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{e^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}dtd\tau d\xi dy\Bigg(\bigg|(D_{y}-ID_{\xi}-JD_{t}-KD_{\tau})\mathcal{A}^{a}+I(F_{y\xi}+F_{\tau t})+J(F_{yt}+F_{\xi\tau})

(6.8)

\displaystyle+K(F_{y\tau}+F_{t\xi})+q^{a}\bigg|^{2}+\dots\Bigg)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{e^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{y}\times\mathbb{R}_{\xi}}d\xi dy\int_{\mathbb{R}_{\tau}\times\mathbb{R}_{t}}dtd\tau\Bigg(\bigg|(D_{y}-ID_{\xi})\mathcal{A}^{a}+P^{a}+IF_{y\xi}+J(D_{y}A_{t}+D_{\xi}A_{\tau})

\displaystyle+K(D_{y}A_{\tau}-D_{\xi}A_{t})+Q+q^{a}\Bigg)\,.

Here
\mathcal{A}^{a}
and
a
are coordinates and indices on the space
\mathfrak{A}_{4}
or irreducible
\mathcal{A}_{w}
fields on
CY_{2}
, and

P^{a}=-JD_{t}\mathcal{A}^{a}-KD_{\tau}\mathcal{A}^{a}\,,\qquad Q=IF_{\tau t}-J(\partial_{t}A_{y}+\partial_{\tau}A_{\xi})-K(\partial_{\tau}A_{y}-\partial_{t}A_{\xi})\,,

(6.9)

with
q^{a}
corresponding to
q
in (
6.7
).

In other words, Spin
(7)
theory on
CY_{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{4}
can be regarded as a 4d gauged sigma model along the
(t,\tau,\xi,y)
-directions with target space
\mathfrak{A}_{4}
and action (
6.8
).

Spin
(7)
Theory as a 2d Model

From (
6.8
), one can see that we can, after suitable rescalings, also recast the 4d model action as an equivalent 2d model action
56
56

56

Just like in
footnote 13
, to arrive at the following expression, we have employed Stokes’ theorem and the fact that the finite-energy gauge fields
A_{\{t,\tau,\xi,y\}}
would vanish at
t,\tau\rightarrow\pm\infty
.

\displaystyle S_{\text{2d},\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{t}\times\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{A}_{4})}=\frac{1}{e^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}d\xi dy\Bigg(\bigg|

\displaystyle(D_{y}-ID_{\xi})\mathcal{A}^{m}+P^{m}+IF_{y\xi}+J\left(D_{y}(\tilde{A}_{t})^{m}+D_{\xi}(\tilde{A}_{\tau})^{m}\right)

(6.10)

\displaystyle+K\left(D_{y}(\tilde{A}_{\tau})^{m}-D_{\xi}(\tilde{A}_{t})^{m}\right)+q^{m}\bigg|^{2}+\dots\Bigg)\,.

Here,
(\mathcal{A}^{m},(\tilde{A}_{\tau})^{m},(\tilde{A}_{t})^{m})
and
m
are coordinates and indices on the double path space
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{t}\times\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{A}_{4})
of smooth double paths from
\mathbb{R}_{t}\times\mathbb{R}_{\tau}
to
\mathfrak{A}_{4}
, with

P^{m}=-J(\tilde{D}_{t}\mathcal{A})^{m}-K(\tilde{D}_{\tau}\mathcal{A})^{m}

(6.11)

corresponding to
P^{a}
in (
6.9
),
q^{m}
corresponding to
q^{a}
, and
(\tilde{A}_{t},\tilde{A}_{\tau},\tilde{D}_{t},\tilde{D}_{\tau})
corresponding to
(A_{t},A_{\tau},D_{t},D_{\tau})
, in the underlying 4d model.

In other words, Spin
(7)
theory on
CY_{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{4}
can also be regarded as a 2d gauged sigma model along the
(\xi,y)
-directions with target space
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{t}\times\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{A}_{4})
and action (
6.10
).

Spin
(7)
Theory as a 1d SQM

Singling out
y
as the direction in “time”, the equivalent SQM action can be obtained from (
6.10
) after suitable rescalings as
57
57

57

In the resulting SQM, we have integrated out
A_{y}
(for a similar reason explained in
footnote 14
) and applied Stokes’ theorem and the fact that the terms containing the fields corresponding to
A_{\{t,\tau,\xi\}}
would vanish at
\xi\rightarrow\pm\infty
.

S_{\text{SQM},\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{\xi},\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{t}\times\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{A}_{4}))}=\frac{1}{e^{2}}\int dy\left(\left|(\partial_{y}\mathcal{A}^{u}+\partial_{y}\widehat{A}^{u})+g^{uv}_{\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{\xi},\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{t}\times\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{A}_{4}))}\left(\frac{\partial\mathcal{H}_{4}}{\partial\mathcal{A}^{u}}+\frac{\partial\mathcal{H}_{4}}{\partial\widehat{A}^{u}}\right)\right|^{2}+\dots\right)\,,

(6.12)

where
(\mathcal{A}^{u},\widehat{A}^{u})
and
(u,v)
are coordinates and indices on the path space
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{\xi},\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{t}\times\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{A}_{4}))
of smooth maps from
\mathbb{R}_{\xi}
to
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{t}\times\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{A}_{4})
with
\widehat{A}^{u}\coloneqq-(I\widehat{A}_{\xi}+J\widehat{A}_{t}+K\widehat{A}_{\tau})^{u}
in
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{\xi},\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau}\times\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{A}_{4}))
corresponding to
-(IA_{\xi}+J(\tilde{A}_{t})^{m}+K(\widetilde{A}_{\tau})^{m})
in the underlying 2d model, and to
-(IA_{\xi}+JA_{t}+KA_{\tau})
in the underlying 4d model;

g_{\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{\xi},\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{t}\times\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{A}_{4}))}
is the metric of
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{\xi},\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{t}\times\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{A}_{4}))
;
and
\mathcal{H}_{4}(\mathcal{A},\widehat{A})
is the SQM potential function.
Note that we can also interpret
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{\xi},\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{t}\times\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{A}_{4}))
as the
triple
path space
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}^{3},\mathfrak{A}_{4})
of smooth maps from
\mathbb{R}^{3}
to
\mathfrak{A}_{4}
.

In short, Spin
(7)
theory on
CY_{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{4}
can also be regarded as a 1d SQM along the
y
-direction in
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}^{3},\mathfrak{A}_{4})
whose action is (
6.12
).

6.2
Non-constant Paths, Solitons, Threebranes, and the HW Floer Homology of
CY_{2}

By a similar analysis to that which was done in

§

3.2
, we find that the equivalent 1d SQM of Spin
(7)
theory on
CY_{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{4}
will localize onto
y
-invariant,
\theta
-deformed, non-constant
triple paths in
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{\xi},\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{t}\times\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{A}_{4}))
which will correspond, in the 2d gauged sigma model with target space
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{t}\times\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{A}_{4})
, to
y
-invariant,
\theta
-deformed
solitons along the
\xi
-direction that we shall refer to as
\mathcal{M}^{\theta}(\mathbb{R}_{t}\times\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{A}_{4})
-solitons.

\mathcal{M}^{\theta}(\mathbb{R}_{t}\times\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{A}_{4})
-solitons in the 2d Gauged Model

Specifically, such
\mathcal{M}^{\theta}(\mathbb{R}_{t}\times\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{A}_{4})
-solitons are defined by

\displaystyle I\partial_{\xi}\left(\mathcal{A}^{m}+A_{y}+J(\tilde{A}_{t})^{m}+K(\tilde{A}_{\tau})^{m}\right)=

\displaystyle-I[A_{\xi},\mathcal{A}^{m}+A_{y}+J(\tilde{A}_{t})^{m}+K(\tilde{A}_{\tau})^{m}]

(6.13)

\displaystyle+[A_{y},\mathcal{A}^{m}+J(\tilde{A}_{t})^{m}+K(\tilde{A}_{\tau})^{m}]

\displaystyle-J(\tilde{D}_{t}\mathcal{A})^{m}-K(\tilde{D}_{\tau}\mathcal{A})^{m}+q^{m}\,.

\mathfrak{A}_{4}^{\theta}
-threebranes in the 4d Gauged Model

In turn, they will correspond, in the 4d gauged sigma model with target space
\mathfrak{A}_{4}
, to
y
-invariant,
\theta
-deformed threebranes along the
(\xi,\tau,t)
-directions that are defined by

\displaystyle(I\partial_{\xi}+J\partial_{t}+K\partial_{\tau})(\mathcal{A}^{a}+A_{y})-I(\partial_{\tau}A_{t}-\partial_{t}A_{\tau})-J(\partial_{\xi}A_{\tau}-\partial_{\tau}A_{\xi})-K(\partial_{t}A_{\xi}-\partial_{\xi}A_{t})

(6.14)

\displaystyle=-[IA_{\xi}+JA_{t}+KA_{\tau},\mathcal{A}^{a}+A_{y}]+[A_{y},\mathcal{A}^{a}]+I[A_{\tau},A_{t}]+J[A_{\xi},A_{\tau}]+K[A_{t},A_{\xi}]+q^{a}\,,

and the condition

\mathfrak{g}_{4}(p)=0\,,

(6.15)

where
\mathfrak{g}_{4}(p)
is the auxiliary scalar field defined in
footnote 55
.
We shall refer to such threebranes defined by (
6.14
) and (
6.15
) as
\mathfrak{A}_{4}^{\theta}
-threebranes.

y
-independent,
\theta
-deformed Spin
(7)
Configurations on Spin
(7)
Theory

In turn, the 4d configurations defined by (
6.14
) and (
6.15
) will correspond, in Spin
(7)
theory, to
y
-independent,
\theta
-deformed Spin
(7)
configurations on
CY_{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{4}
that are defined, via (
6.7
), by

\displaystyle(I\partial_{\xi}+J\partial_{t}+K\partial_{\tau})(\mathcal{A}_{\kappa}+A_{y})-I(\partial_{\tau}A_{t}-\partial_{t}A_{\tau})-J(\partial_{\xi}A_{\tau}-\partial_{\tau}A_{\xi})-K(\partial_{t}A_{\xi}-\partial_{\xi}A_{t})

(6.16)

\displaystyle=-\mathcal{D}_{\kappa}(A_{y}-IA_{\xi}-JA_{t}-KA_{\tau})-[IA_{\xi}+JA_{t}+KA_{\tau},A_{y}]+I[A_{\tau},A_{t}]+J[A_{\xi},A_{\tau}]

\displaystyle+K[A_{t},A_{\xi}]-4K_{\theta}\varepsilon^{\kappa\lambda}\mathcal{F}_{\kappa\lambda}\,,

and the condition

2Ie^{-I\theta}\omega^{\kappa\bar{\lambda}}\mathcal{F}_{\kappa\bar{\lambda}}=0\,.

(6.17)

Spin
(7)
Configurations,
\mathfrak{A}_{4}^{\theta}
-threebranes,
\mathcal{M}^{\theta}(\mathbb{R}_{t}\times\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{A}_{4})
-solitons, and Non-constant Triple Paths

In short, these
y
-independent,
\theta
-deformed
Spin
(7)
configurations on
CY_{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{4}
that are defined by (
6.16
) and (
6.17
), will correspond to the
\mathfrak{A}_{4}^{\theta}
-threebranes defined by (
6.14
) and (
6.15
), which, in turn, will correspond to the
\mathcal{M}^{\theta}(\mathbb{R}_{t}\times\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{A}_{4})
-solitons defined by (
6.13
), which, in turn, will correspond to the
y
-invariant,
\theta
-deformed, non-constant triple paths in
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}^{3},\mathfrak{A}_{4})
defined by setting both the LHS and RHS of the expression within the squared term of (
6.12
)
simultaneously
to zero.

\mathcal{M}^{\theta}(\mathbb{R}_{t}\times\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{A}_{4})
-soliton Endpoints Corresponding to Non-constant Double Paths

Consider now the fixed endpoints of the
\mathcal{M}^{\theta}(\mathbb{R}_{t}\times\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{A}_{4})
-solitons at
\xi=\pm\infty
, where we also expect the fields in the 2d gauged sigma model corresponding to the finite-energy 4d gauge fields
A_{y},A_{\xi},A_{\tau},A_{t}
to decay to zero.
They are given by (
6.13
) with
\partial_{\xi}\mathcal{A}^{m}=0
and
A_{y},A_{\xi},(\tilde{A}_{\tau})^{m},(\tilde{A}_{t})^{m}\rightarrow 0
, i.e.,

J(\tilde{\partial}_{t}\mathcal{A})^{m}+K(\tilde{\partial}_{\tau}\mathcal{A})^{m}=q^{m}\,.

(6.18)

These are
(y,\xi)
-invariant,
\theta
-deformed, non-constant
double
paths in
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{t}\times\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{A}_{4})
.

\mathfrak{A}_{4}^{\theta}
-threebrane Faces Corresponding to
\mathfrak{A}_{4}^{\theta}
-sheets in the 4d Gauged Model

In turn, (
6.18
) will correspond, in the 4d gauged sigma model, to the fixed faces of the
\mathfrak{A}_{4}^{\theta}
-threebranes at
\xi=\pm\infty
, i.e.,
(y,\xi)
-invariant,
\theta
-deformed sheets along the
(\tau,t)
-direction that are defined by

J\partial_{t}\mathcal{A}^{a}+K\partial_{\tau}\mathcal{A}^{a}=q^{a}\,.

(6.19)

Notice that these equations can also be obtained from (
6.14
) with
\partial_{\xi}\mathcal{A}^{a}=0
and
A_{y},A_{\xi},A_{\tau},A_{t}\rightarrow 0
.
Such sheets defined by (
6.19
) and (
6.15
) are
\mathfrak{A}_{4}^{\theta}
-sheets.

\mathfrak{A}_{4}^{\theta}
-threebrane or
\mathfrak{A}_{4}^{\theta}
-sheet Edges Corresponding to
\mathfrak{A}_{4}^{\theta}
-solitons in the 4d Gauged Model

Consider now the fixed edges of (i) the
\mathfrak{A}_{4}^{\theta}
-threebranes at
\xi,\tau=\pm\infty
, or equivalently (ii) the
\mathfrak{A}_{4}^{\theta}
-sheets at
\tau=\pm\infty
.
These are
(y,\xi,\tau)
-invariant,
\theta
-deformed solitons along the
t
-direction and are given by (i) (
6.14
) with
\partial_{\{\tau,\xi\}}\mathcal{A}^{a}=0
and
A_{y},A_{\xi},A_{\tau},A_{t}\rightarrow 0
, or equivalently (ii) (
6.19
) with
\partial_{\tau}\mathcal{A}^{a}=0
, i.e.,

J\partial_{t}\mathcal{A}^{a}=q^{a}\,.

(6.20)

Such solitons defined by (
6.20
) and (
6.15
) are
\mathfrak{A}_{4}^{\theta}
-solitons.

\mathfrak{A}_{4}^{\theta}
-soliton Endpoints, and
\mathfrak{A}_{4}^{\theta}
-sheet or
\mathfrak{A}_{4}^{\theta}
-threebrane Vertices Corresponding to
\theta
-deformed VW Configurations on
CY_{2}

Consider now (i) the fixed endpoints of the
\mathfrak{A}_{4}^{\theta}
-solitons at
t=\pm\infty
, (ii) the vertices of the
\mathfrak{A}_{4}^{\theta}
-sheets at
\tau,t=\pm\infty
, or equivalently (iii) the vertices of the
\mathfrak{A}_{4}^{\theta}
-threebranes at
\xi,\tau,t=\pm\infty
.
They are given by (i) (
6.20
) and (
6.15
) with
\partial_{t}\mathcal{A}^{a}=0
, (ii) (
6.19
) and (
6.15
) with
\partial_{\{t,\tau\}}\mathcal{A}^{a}=0
, or equivalently (iii) (
6.14
) and (
6.15
) with
\partial_{\{t,\tau,\xi\}}\mathcal{A}^{a}=0
and
A_{y},A_{\xi},A_{\tau},A_{t}\rightarrow 0
.
In turn, they will correspond, in Spin
(7)
theory, to
(y,\xi,\tau,t)
-independent,
\theta
-deformed configurations that obey (
6.16
) and (
6.17
) with
\partial_{\{t,\tau,\xi\}}\mathcal{A}^{a}=0
and
A_{y},A_{\xi},A_{\tau},A_{t}\rightarrow 0
, i.e.,

K_{\theta}\varepsilon^{\kappa\lambda}\mathcal{F}_{\kappa\lambda}=0\,,\qquad Ie^{-I\theta}\omega^{\kappa\bar{\lambda}}\mathcal{F}_{\kappa\bar{\lambda}}=0\,.

(6.21)

At
\theta=0,\pi
, (
6.21
) can be written, in the real coordinates of
CY_{2}
, as

F_{\alpha\beta}^{+}=0\,.

(6.22)

This is the ASD instanton equation on
CY_{2}
, which can be interpreted as VW equations on
CY_{2}
with the self-dual two-form field being zero (i.e., (
5.18
) with
B=0
).
We shall, in the rest of this section, refer to configurations that span the space of solutions to this equation as VW configurations on
CY_{2}
.
Recall also, from

§

5.2
, that VW configurations on
CY_{2}
are known to generate the HW Floer homology of
CY_{2}
.

In other words, the
(y,\xi,\tau,t)
-independent,
\theta
-deformed Spin
(7)
configurations corresponding to the endpoints of the
\mathfrak{A}_{4}^{\theta}
-solitons (or equivalently, the vertices of the
\mathfrak{A}_{4}^{\theta}
-sheets and
\mathfrak{A}_{4}^{\theta}
-threebranes) are
\theta
-deformed VW configurations on
CY_{2}
.
We will also assume choices of
CY_{2}
satisfying
footnote 45
whereby such configurations are isolated and non-degenerate.

Non-constant Triple Paths,
\mathcal{M}^{\theta}(\mathbb{R}_{t}\times\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{A}_{4})
-solitons,
\mathfrak{A}_{4}^{\theta}
-solitons,
\mathfrak{A}_{4}^{\theta}
-sheets, and
\mathfrak{A}_{4}^{\theta}
-threebranes.

In short, from the equivalent 1d SQM of Spin
(7)
theory on
CY_{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{4}
, the theory localizes onto
y
-invariant,
\theta
-deformed, non-constant triple paths in
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}^{3},\mathfrak{A}_{4})
, which, in turn, will correspond to
\mathcal{M}^{\theta}(\mathbb{R}_{t}\times\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{A}_{4})
-solitons in the 2d gauged sigma model whose endpoints are
(y,\xi)
-invariant,
\theta
-deformed, non-constant double paths in
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{t}\times\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{A}_{4})
.
In the 4d gauged sigma model, these
\mathcal{M}^{\theta}(\mathbb{R}_{t}\times\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{A}_{4})
-solitons will correspond to
\mathfrak{A}_{4}^{\theta}
-threebranes, whose faces are
\mathfrak{A}_{4}^{\theta}
-sheets, whose edges are
\mathfrak{A}_{4}^{\theta}
-solitons, and whose vertices will correspond to
\theta
-deformed VW configurations on
CY_{2}
that generate the HW Floer homology of
CY_{2}
.

6.3
The 2d Model and Open Strings, the 4d Model and Open Threebranes

By following a similar analysis to that which was done in

§

3.3
, we find that the 2d gauged sigma model with target space
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{t}\times\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{A}_{4})
whose action is (
6.10
), will define an open string theory in
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{t}\times\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{A}_{4})
.
Similarly, we find that the 4d gauged sigma model with target space
\mathfrak{A}_{4}
whose action is (
6.8
) will define an open
threebrane
theory in
\mathfrak{A}_{4}
.
We will now work out the details pertaining to the BPS worldsheets and BPS worldvolumes (including their boundaries) that are necessary to define this open string theory and open threebrane theory, respectively.

BPS Worldsheets of the 2d Model

The BPS worldsheets of the 2d gauged sigma model with target space
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{t}\times\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{A}_{4})
correspond to its classical trajectories.
Specifically, these are defined by setting to zero the expression within the squared term in (
6.10
), i.e.,

\frac{D\mathcal{A}^{m}}{Dy}-I\frac{D\mathcal{A}^{m}}{D\xi}+IF_{y\xi}+J\left(\frac{D(\tilde{A}_{t})^{m}}{Dy}+\frac{D(\tilde{A}_{\tau})^{m}}{D\xi}\right)+K\left(\frac{D(\tilde{A}_{\tau})^{m}}{Dy}-\frac{D(\tilde{A}_{t})^{m}}{D\xi}\right)=-P^{m}-q^{m}\,.

(6.23)

BPS Worldsheets with Boundaries Labeled by Non-constant Double Paths in
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{t}\times\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{A}_{4})

The boundaries of the BPS worldsheets are traced out by the endpoints of the
\mathcal{M}^{\theta}(\mathbb{R}_{t}\times\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{A}_{4})
-solitons as they propagate in
y
.
As we have seen at the end of

§

(y,\xi)
-invariant,
\theta

In turn, they will correspond, in the 4d gauged sigma model with target space

.

Suppose there are
n\geq 8

\{\mathcal{E}^{1}_{\text{VW}}(\theta),\mathcal{E}^{2}_{\text{VW}}(\theta),\dots,\mathcal{E}^{n}_{\text{VW}}(\theta)\}

6.4
Soliton String Theory, the Spin
(7)
Partition Function, and an FS type
A_{\infty}
-category of
\mathfrak{A}_{4}^{\theta}
-sheets

The 2d Model as a 2d Gauged LG Model

Notice that we can also express (
6.23
) as

\frac{D\mathcal{A}^{m}}{Dy}-I\frac{D\mathcal{A}^{m}}{D\xi}+\frac{D\breve{A}^{m}}{Dy}-I\frac{D\breve{A}^{m}}{D\xi}+IF_{y\xi}=-P^{m}-q^{m}\,.

(6.25)

Here
\mathcal{A}^{m}
and
\breve{A}^{m}\coloneq J(\tilde{A}_{t})^{m}+K(\tilde{A}_{\tau})^{m}
can be interpreted as holomorphic coordinates on the complex path space
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{C},\mathfrak{A}_{4})
of paths from the
I
-complexified
\mathbb{R}_{t}\times\mathbb{R}_{\tau}
to
\mathfrak{A}_{4}
.
In turn, this means that we can express the action of the 2d gauged sigma model with target space
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{t}\times\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{A}_{4})
in (
6.10
) as

\displaystyle S_{\text{2d-LG},\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{C},\mathfrak{A}_{4})}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{e^{2}}\int d\xi dy\Bigg(\bigg|(D_{y}-ID_{\xi})\left(\mathcal{A}^{m}+\breve{A}^{m}\right)+IF_{y\xi}+P^{m}+q^{m}\bigg|^{2}+\dots\Bigg)

(6.26)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{e^{2}}\int d\xi dy\Bigg(\bigg|(D_{y}-ID_{\xi})\left(\mathcal{A}^{m}+\breve{A}^{m}\right)+IF_{y\xi}+g^{m\bar{n}}_{\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{C},\mathfrak{A}_{4})}\left(\frac{K\zeta}{2}\frac{\partial\mathfrak{w}_{4}}{\partial\mathcal{A}^{n}}\right)^{*}

\displaystyle+g^{m\bar{n}}_{\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{C},\mathfrak{A}_{4})}\left(\frac{K\zeta}{2}\frac{\partial\mathfrak{w}_{4}}{\partial\breve{A}^{n}}\right)^{*}\bigg|^{2}+\dots\Bigg)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{e^{2}}\int d\xi dy\Bigg(|D_{\rho}\mathcal{A}^{m}|^{2}+|D_{\rho}\breve{A}^{m}|^{2}+\left|\frac{\partial\mathfrak{w}_{4}}{\partial\mathcal{A}^{m}}\right|^{2}+\left|\frac{\partial\mathfrak{w}_{4}}{\partial\breve{A}^{m}}\right|^{2}+|F_{y\xi}|^{2}+\dots\Bigg)\,,

where
g_{\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{C},\mathfrak{A}_{4})}
is the metric on
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{C},\mathfrak{A}_{4})
and
\zeta\coloneqq e^{I\theta}
.
In other words, the 2d gauged sigma model with target space
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{t}\times\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{A}_{4})
can also be interpreted as a 2d gauged LG model with target space
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{C},\mathfrak{A}_{4})
and a holomorphic superpotential
\mathfrak{w}_{4}(\mathcal{A},\breve{A})
.

By setting
d_{y}\mathcal{A}^{m}=0
and
A_{y},A_{\xi},\breve{A}^{m}\rightarrow 0
in the expression within the squared term in (
6.26
), we can read off the LG
\mathcal{M}^{\theta}(\mathbb{C},\mathfrak{A}_{4})
-soliton equation corresponding to
\Xi^{\{IJ,KL\},\{MN,PQ\}}_{\pm}(\xi,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{4})
(that re-expresses (
6.13
) with
A_{y},A_{\xi},(\tilde{A}_{t})^{m},(\tilde{A}_{\tau})^{m}\rightarrow 0
) as

I\frac{d\mathcal{A}^{m}}{d\xi}=g^{m\bar{n}}_{\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{C},\mathfrak{A}_{4})}\left(\frac{K\zeta}{2}\frac{\partial\mathfrak{w}_{4}}{\partial\mathcal{A}^{n}}\right)^{*}_{\breve{A}=0}+g^{m\bar{n}}_{\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{C},\mathfrak{A}_{4})}\left(\frac{K\zeta}{2}\frac{\partial\mathfrak{w}_{4}}{\partial(\breve{A})^{n}}\right)^{*}_{\breve{A}=0}\,.

(6.27)

By setting
d_{\xi}\mathcal{A}^{m}=0
in (
6.27
), we get the LG
\mathcal{M}^{\theta}(\mathbb{C},\mathfrak{A}_{4})
-soliton endpoint equation corresponding to
\Xi^{\{IJ,KL\},\{MN,PQ\}}(\pm\infty,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{4})
(that re-expresses (
6.18
)) as

g^{m\bar{n}}_{\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{C},\mathfrak{A}_{4})}\left(\frac{K\zeta}{2}\frac{\partial\mathfrak{w}_{4}}{\partial\mathcal{A}^{n}}\right)^{*}_{\breve{A}=0}+g^{m\bar{n}}_{\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{C},\mathfrak{A}_{4})}\left(\frac{K\zeta}{2}\frac{\partial\mathfrak{w}_{4}}{\partial\breve{A}^{n}}\right)^{*}_{\breve{A}=0}=0\,.

(6.28)

Recall from the end of

§

6.2
that we are only considering certain
CY_{2}
such that the
\theta
-deformed VW configurations are isolated and non-degenerate.
Next, recall also that such configurations will correspond to the vertices of the
\mathfrak{A}_{4}^{\theta}
-sheets; therefore, just like their vertices, these
\mathfrak{A}_{4}^{\theta}
-sheets would be isolated and non-degenerate.
As these
\mathfrak{A}_{4}^{\theta}
-sheets will correspond, in the 2d gauged sigma model, to the endpoints of the
\mathcal{M}^{\theta}(\mathbb{R}_{t}\times\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{A}_{4})
-solitons (and thus the endpoints of the LG
\mathcal{M}^{\theta}(\mathbb{C},\mathfrak{A}_{4})
-solitons), i.e.,
\Xi^{\{IJ,KL\},\{MN,PQ\}}(\pm\infty,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{4})
, this means that the latter would also be isolated and non-degenerate.
Thus, from their definition in (
6.28
) which tells us that they are critical points of
\mathfrak{w}_{4}(\mathcal{A},\breve{A})
, we conclude that
\mathfrak{w}_{4}(\mathcal{A},\breve{A})
can be regarded as a holomorphic Morse function in
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{C},\mathfrak{A}_{4})
.

Similar to

§

3.4
, an LG
\mathcal{M}^{\theta}(\mathbb{C},\mathfrak{A}_{4})
-soliton defined in (
6.27
) maps to a straight line in the complex
\mathfrak{w}_{4}
-plane that starts and ends at the critical points
\mathfrak{w}_{4}^{IJ,KL}(\theta)\equiv\mathfrak{w}_{4}\left(\Xi^{\{IJ,KL\},\{MN,PQ\}}(-\infty,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{4})\right)
and
\mathfrak{w}_{4}^{MN,PQ}(\theta)\equiv\mathfrak{w}_{4}\left(\Xi^{\{IJ,KL\},\{MN,PQ\}}(+\infty,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{4})\right)
, respectively, where its slope depends on
\theta
(via
\zeta
).
We shall also assume that
\text{Re}(\mathfrak{w}_{4}^{IJ,KL}(\theta))<\text{Re}(\mathfrak{w}_{4}^{MN,PQ}(\theta))
and that
I\neq J
,
K\neq L
,
J\neq K
,
M\neq N
,
P\neq Q
, and
N\neq P
.

The 2d Gauged LG Model as an LG SQM

With suitable rescalings, we can recast (
6.26
) as a 1d LG SQM (that re-expresses (
6.12
)), where its action will be given by
60
60

60

In the following expression, we have integrated out
A_{y}
and omitted the fields corresponding to the finite-energy gauge fields
A_{\{t,\tau,\xi\}}
(as explained in
footnote 57
).

S_{\text{2d-LG SQM},\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{\xi},\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{C},\mathfrak{A}_{4}))}=\frac{1}{e^{2}}\int dy\left(\left|\left(\frac{d\mathcal{A}^{u}}{dy}+\frac{d\widehat{A}^{u}}{dy}\right)+g^{uv}_{\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{\xi},\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{C},\mathfrak{A}_{4}))}\left(\frac{\partial\mathfrak{H}_{4}}{\partial\mathcal{A}^{u}}+\frac{\partial\mathfrak{H}_{4}}{\partial\widehat{A}^{u}}\right)\right|^{2}+\dots\right)\,,

(6.29)

where
\mathfrak{H}_{4}(\mathcal{A},\widehat{A})
is the
real-valued
potential in
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{\xi},\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{C},\mathfrak{A}_{4}))
, and the subscript “
2d-LG SQM
,
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{\xi},\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{C},\mathfrak{A}_{4}))
” is to specify that it is a 1d SQM in
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{\xi},\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{C},\mathfrak{A}_{4}))
obtained from the equivalent 2d LG model.
We will also refer to this
1d
LG SQM as “2d-LG SQM” in the rest of this subsection.

The 2d-LG SQM will localize onto configurations that
simultaneously
set to zero the LHS and RHS of the expression within the squared term in (
6.29
).
In other words, it will localize onto
y
-invariant critical points of
\mathfrak{H}_{4}(\mathcal{A},\widehat{A})
that will correspond, when
A_{y},A_{\xi},\breve{A}^{m}\rightarrow 0
, to the LG
\mathcal{M}^{\theta}(\mathbb{C},\mathfrak{A}_{4})
-solitons defined by (
6.27
).
For our choice of
CY_{2}
, the LG
\mathcal{M}^{\theta}(\mathbb{C},\mathfrak{A}_{4})
-solitons, just like their endpoints, will be isolated and non-degenerate.
Thus,
\mathfrak{H}_{4}(\mathcal{A},\widehat{A})
can be regarded as a real-valued Morse functional in
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{\xi},\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{C},\mathfrak{A}_{4}))
.

Morphisms from
\varsigma^{IJ,KL}(\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{4})
to
\varsigma^{MN,PQ}(\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{4})
as Floer Homology Classes of Intersecting Thimbles

Repeating here the analysis in

§

3.4
with (
6.29
) as the action of the 2d-LG SQM, we find that we can interpret the LG
\mathcal{M}^{\theta}(\mathbb{C},\mathfrak{A}_{4})
-soliton solution
\Xi^{\{IJ,KL\},\{MN,PQ\}}_{\pm}(\xi,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{4})
as intersections of thimbles.
Specifically, a
\Xi^{\{IJ,KL\},\{MN,PQ\}}_{\pm}(\xi,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{4})
-soliton pair, whose left and right endpoints correspond to
\varsigma^{IJ,KL}(\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{4})
and
\varsigma^{MN,PQ}(\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{4})
, respectively, can be identified as a pair of intersection points
\mathfrak{p}^{\{IJ,KL\},\{MN,PQ\}}_{\text{VW},\pm}(\theta)\in\big(S^{IJ}_{\text{VW}}\cap S^{KL}_{\text{VW}}\big)\bigcap\big(S^{MN}_{\text{VW}}\cap S^{PQ}_{\text{VW}}\big)
of a left and right thimble in the fiber space over the line segment
\big[\mathfrak{w}_{4}^{IJ,KL}(\theta),\mathfrak{w}_{4}^{MN,PQ}(\theta)\big]
.

This means that the 2d-LG SQM in
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{\xi},\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{C},\mathfrak{A}_{4}))
with action (
6.29
), will physically realize a Floer homology that we shall name an
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{C},\mathfrak{A}_{4})
-LG Floer homology.
The chains of the
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{C},\mathfrak{A}_{4})
-LG Floer complex will be generated by LG
\mathcal{M}^{\theta}(\mathbb{C},\mathfrak{A}_{4})
-solitons which we can identify with
\mathfrak{p}^{\{**,**\},\{**,**\}}_{\text{VW},\pm}(\theta)
, and the
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{C},\mathfrak{A}_{4})
-LG Floer differential will be realized by the flow lines governed by the gradient flow equations satisfied by the
y
-varying configurations that set the expression within the squared term in (
6.29
) to zero.
The partition function of the 2d-LG SQM in
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{\xi},\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{C},\mathfrak{A}_{4}))
will then be given by
61
61

61

The ‘
\theta
’ label is omitted in the LHS of the following expression (as explained in

).

\mathcal{Z}_{\text{2d-LG SQM},\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{\xi},\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{C},\mathfrak{A}_{4}))}(G)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\neq J\neq K\neq L\neq M\\
\neq N\neq P\neq Q=1\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathfrak{p}^{\{IJ,KL\},\{MN,PQ\}}_{\text{VW},\pm}\\
\in\left(S^{IJ}_{\text{VW}}\cap S^{KL}_{\text{VW}}\right)\bigcap\left(S^{MN}_{\text{VW}}\cap S^{PQ}_{\text{VW}}\right)\end{subarray}}\text{HF}^{G}_{d_{v}}\left(\mathfrak{p}^{\{IJ,KL\},\{MN,PQ\}}_{\text{VW},\pm}(\theta)\right)\,.

(6.30)

Here, the contribution
\text{HF}^{G}_{d_{v}}(\mathfrak{p}^{\{IJ,KL\},\{MN,PQ\}}_{\text{VW},\pm}(\theta))
can be identified with a homology class in an

\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{C},\mathfrak{A}_{4})

in

which can also be identified as
\mathfrak{p}^{\{IJ,KL\},\{MN,PQ\}}_{\text{VW},\pm}(\theta)
.

Therefore,
\mathcal{Z}_{\text{2d-LG SQM},\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{\xi},\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{C},\mathfrak{A}_{4}))}(G)

and
\varsigma^{MN,PQ}(\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{4})

In other words, we can write

\text{CF}_{\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{t}\times\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{A}_{4})}\left(\varsigma^{IJ,KL}(\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{4}),\varsigma^{MN,PQ}(\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{4})\right)_{\pm}=\text{HF}^{G}_{d_{v}}\left(\mathfrak{p}^{\{IJ,KL\},\{MN,PQ\}}_{\text{VW},\pm}(\theta)\right)\,,

(6.31)

where
\text{CF}_{\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}_{t}\times\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{A}_{4})}(\varsigma^{IJ,KL}(\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{4}),\varsigma^{MN,PQ}(\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{4}))_{\pm}
is a vector representing a
\Xi^{\{IJ,KL\},\{MN,PQ\}}(\xi,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{4})
-soliton, such that
\text{Re}(\mathfrak{w}_{4}^{IJ,KL}(\theta))<\text{Re}(\mathfrak{w}_{4}^{MN,PQ}(\theta))
.
This will lead us to the following one-to-one identification

The ‘
\theta
’ label is omitted in the following expression (as explained in

).

\boxed{\text{Hom}\left(\varsigma^{IJ,KL}(\mathfrak{A}_{4}),\varsigma^{MN,PQ}(\mathfrak{A}_{4})\right)_{\pm}\Longleftrightarrow\text{HF}^{G}_{d_{v}}\left(\mathfrak{p}^{\{IJ,KL\},\{MN,PQ\}}_{\text{VW},\pm}\right)}

where the RHS is (i) proportional to the identity class when

, and
